  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Casimir III, leader of Poland (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Poland
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Winged Hussar, Replacing Lancer (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Pancerny, Replacing Heavy Skirmisher (Requires technology: Physics)
        - Unique Building: Ducal Stable, Replacing Stable (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Building: Barbican, Replacing Armory (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Civilization Ability: Gain 1 Free Social Policy when you enter the Classical, Renaissance, Modern and Information Eras. Gain 2 additional Free Tenets when you adopt an Ideology for the first time.
      - Leader: Casimir III
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      
      ## Policies
      - Transnationalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Transnationalism: Each turn, increases the chance that a Corporate Franchise will appear in a foreign City to 15% (normally 5%). Global Franchise maximum increased by 25%, and every owned Town you have built counts as a Franchise. +2 Culture from all Corporation Offices.
      - Universal Healthcare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Universal Healthcare: Receive a free Hospital in every City. +50 when a Citizen is born in any City, scaling with Era.
      - Their Finest Hour (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Their Finest Hour: All cities get +2 Air Unit Slots. Each Air Unit stationed in a city increases the City's Defense by 3. Can build B17 Bombers.
      - Arsenal of Democracy (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Arsenal of Democracy: +15% Production towards Military Units. +10 Influence with all known City-States when you expend Great People, and +40 Influence when you gift Units to City-States.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Self Determination (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Self Determination: Liberating a city (including removing another civ's Sphere of Influence on a City-State through conquest) gives 15 XP to all units, 50 Influence with all City-States, and 40 Science, scaling with Era and City Population. The liberated city gains an Arsenal and 6 units.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 5
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 8

        - DeceptiveBias: 4
        - Rationale: Tweaked by In-Game AI (Turn 364: Adjusting persona for wartime. Russia's declaration forces us from peaceful science mode to defensive war posture. Boldness:5->8 to resist Russian aggression, WarBias:1->6 to enable counter-offensives, HostileBias:3->8 to treat Russia as mortal threat, FriendlyBias:10->3 because ideologically aligned 'allies' like Inca betrayed us with Cold War proposal. DiplomaticBalance:10->5 - no more appeasement, survival requires decisive action.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Computers
        - Rationale: Turn 372: After completing Nuclear Fission (5 turns remaining), Computers is essential. It grants +1 Trade Route and unlocks Carrier for air power projection. More importantly, Computers leads to Telecommunications → Satellites → Nanotechnology → Future Tech - the critical path for science victory. Combined Arms (Tanks) would help against Russia but we have nuclear deterrent now; closing the tech gap with Korea (67 vs 65) is paramount.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Transnationalism (Policy)
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Maintaining Transnationalism as next Freedom tenet. With trade routes disrupted by war and Babylon-Shoshone peace changing dynamics, the corporate franchise mechanics (15% appearance rate, +25% global max, +2 Culture from Offices) provide economic resilience. After this, consider Arsenal of Democracy for military production if Russia war escalates further.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: 5
        - Private: 5
        - Rationale: Turn 321: Normalizing Rome relationship from -5/-5 to +5/+5. Rome is now a vassal of Inca (Freedom-aligned), creating bloc solidarity. They maintain DoF with us and are distant non-threats. The previous negative modifiers reflected their vassal obligations during Inca-Russia tensions, but with Inca adopting Freedom and ideological alignment achieved, positive relations with Rome support coalition cohesion. Sanctions against Rome are a World Congress matter; bilateral relations should remain constructive.
        - UpdatedTurn: 321
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Turn 356: Resetting Polynesia relationship from +10/+10 to 0/0. Critical development: Polynesia has capitulated to Shoshone and is now their vassal (Peace Treaty, Master relationship visible). Polynesia is currently at war with our allies Babylon, Korea, and Inca as part of Shoshone's Order bloc. Maintaining positive relations with a vassal of our main enemy is diplomatically inconsistent and could confuse AI threat assessment. Neutral stance acknowledges their compromised position while avoiding premature war declaration.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ### Segou
        - Public: 10
        - Private: 10
        - Rationale: Turn 360: Increasing relationship with Segou from neutral to +10/+10. Segou just became our ally (switched from Korea) and they are strategically located near Shoshone territory. With Shoshone's Decolonization proposal threatening all city-state influence, having strong positive relations with Segou ensures they remain in our sphere. They're also the proposer of Babylon's Sphere of Influence proposal, so good relations help our coalition coordination.
        - UpdatedTurn: 360
        
        ### The Inca
        - Public: -15
        - Private: -20
        - Rationale: Turn 368: Slightly improving Inca relationship from -20/-25 to -15/-20. While they remain suspect for their Cold War proposal that threatened our trade income, they are Freedom-aligned and voting against Decolonization. Cannot afford to alienate all allies. Their 10 delegates matter for World Congress. Keeping negative modifiers acknowledges their betrayal but allows functional alliance against greater threats (Russia, Shoshone).
        - UpdatedTurn: 368
        
        ### Korea
        - Public: 10
        - Private: 5
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Korea has overtaken us in tech (67 vs 65) making them our primary Science Victory competitor. However, they remain critical Freedom ally with 10 World Congress votes for our World Ideology: Freedom proposal in 9 turns. Raising from +5/0 to +10/+5 - we need their votes and they remain ideologically aligned, but we must acknowledge the tech competition.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ### Babylon
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Babylon remains our staunchest Freedom ally with 16 World Congress delegates. They just made peace with Shoshone - this reduces immediate military pressure but we need to ensure they still support our World Ideology: Freedom proposal. Their cultural victory progress (5 influential civs) is secondary concern. Strengthening bond to +40/+45 for World Congress coordination.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ### Russia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Russia continues aggressive war despite our nuclear capability. They have superior military (6111 vs 4591) and are pushing our borders. Their Autocracy ideology vs our Freedom makes them existential threat. Maintaining maximum hostility - we have nuclear weapons now and must use them if they push further into our territory. War score is only -4% but they killed our units near Warsaw.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ### The Shoshone
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -40
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Shoshone made peace with Babylon (critical development). Their massive military (11392) is still a threat but immediate pressure reduced. Order ideology remains opposed to our Freedom. Public neutral to avoid provoking war while we handle Russia, private -40 acknowledges their long-term threat and Decolonization proposal threat to our city-state alliances.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Adjusting for critical science sprint while managing Russian aggression. Korea overtook us in tech (67 vs 65) - maximum Science priority. Increased Defense to 85 as Russia pushing borders aggressively. UseNuke to 90 (from 80) - now have actual nuclear weapons for deterrence. Increased Espionage to 95 to steal tech from Shoshone/Korea to close gap. Reduced Offense to 35 - defensive war only. Maintained high Diplomacy (85) for World Congress Freedom vote in 9 turns. Babylon-Shoshone peace reduces immediate threat but we must prepare for long-term science race.
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 45
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 8 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Polish Sphere of Influence: La Venta:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: The Shoshone:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 326
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 359
          - World Religion: Islam:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Korean Sphere of Influence: Harappa:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 348
          - Sanction: Rome:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Shoshone Sphere of Influence: Cahokia:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 348
          - Open Door: Raqmu:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Babylonian Sphere of Influence: Djenne:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.

        You, Casimir III (leader of Poland, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 373.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Casimir III, leader of Poland (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Poland
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Winged Hussar, Replacing Lancer (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Pancerny, Replacing Heavy Skirmisher (Requires technology: Physics)
        - Unique Building: Ducal Stable, Replacing Stable (Requires technology: Chivalry)
        - Unique Building: Barbican, Replacing Armory (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Civilization Ability: Gain 1 Free Social Policy when you enter the Classical, Renaissance, Modern and Information Eras. Gain 2 additional Free Tenets when you adopt an Ideology for the first time.
      - Leader: Casimir III
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      
      ## Policies
      - Transnationalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Transnationalism: Each turn, increases the chance that a Corporate Franchise will appear in a foreign City to 15% (normally 5%). Global Franchise maximum increased by 25%, and every owned Town you have built counts as a Franchise. +2 Culture from all Corporation Offices.
      - Universal Healthcare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Universal Healthcare: Receive a free Hospital in every City. +50 when a Citizen is born in any City, scaling with Era.
      - Their Finest Hour (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Their Finest Hour: All cities get +2 Air Unit Slots. Each Air Unit stationed in a city increases the City's Defense by 3. Can build B17 Bombers.
      - Arsenal of Democracy (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Arsenal of Democracy: +15% Production towards Military Units. +10 Influence with all known City-States when you expend Great People, and +40 Influence when you gift Units to City-States.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Self Determination (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Self Determination: Liberating a city (including removing another civ's Sphere of Influence on a City-State through conquest) gives 15 XP to all units, 50 Influence with all City-States, and 40 Science, scaling with Era and City Population. The liberated city gains an Arsenal and 6 units.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Opponents lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Set next policy to Avant Garde to boost our Great People generation by 33%. This is critical for generating Great Scientists and Great Engineers to accelerate our progress toward a Spaceship Victory.
        - Policy: Avant Garde
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - Flavors:
          - Gold: 75
          - Offense: 65
          - Spaceship: 100
          - MilitaryTraining: 65
          - Science: 100
          - Production: 95
          - CityDefense: 70
          - Espionage: 95
          - Mobilization: 75
          - Defense: 75
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
      - rationale: Adjusting flavors to support an offensive push against Russia. We have local military dominance around St. Petersburg and a City-State quest to capture it. Raising Offense and Mobilization will encourage our staff to seize this strategic opportunity, while maintaining maximum Science, Production, and Spaceship focus for our ultimate victory goal.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: 15
        - TargetID: 6
        - Rationale: Improve relations with The Inca to solidify our Freedom-based coalition (Poland, Korea, Babylon, Inca) against Russia and the Shoshone, smoothing over past friction from spying and religious differences.
        - Private: 15
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: 15
        - TargetID: 0
        - Rationale: Reinforce our friendship with Korea, our close neighbor and fellow Freedom-aligned nation, ensuring absolute stability on our borders during our war with Russia.
        - Private: 15
    - #4:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 1
        - Public: 20
        - Rationale: Strengthen relations with Babylon to maintain lucrative trade routes and diplomatic alignment. While they are a culture threat, they are actively fighting Russia and serve as a vital buffer and trade partner.
        - Private: 20
  - tokens:

    - outputTokens: 350
  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Planning Next Steps**
            
            I'm currently evaluating potential diplomatic overtures and assessing our internal resource allocation. My focus is on strengthening our economic base to support future military endeavors and solidify our position in the region.
            
            **Exploring Policy Options**
            
            I'm now investigating the impact of various policy choices on our societal development and international standing. My current focus is on how these decisions will influence our long-term strategic goals and resource management.
            
            **Researching Key Technologies**
            
            I'm currently weighing "Computers" against "Combined Arms" as our next research focus. My priority is aligning this choice with our Spaceship victory objective and considering the implications for our military and economic expansion.
            
            **Finalizing Research Decision**
            
            I've confirmed that "Computers" is indeed the optimal research path. This choice directly supports our Spaceship victory conditions by unlocking critical technologies like Satellites.
            
            **Evaluating Ideology Tenets**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our Freedom ideology tenets, considering options like "Universal Healthcare" or "Arsenal of Democracy." My focus is on how these choices best support our Spaceship victory, particularly regarding Great Person generation and military production.
            
            **Choosing Ideology Tweak**
            
            I'm prioritizing Great People generation for our Spaceship victory. I'm shifting our ideology to "Avant Garde" to boost Great Scientists and Engineers, accelerating both research and production.
            
            **Analyzing Combat Events**
            
            I am processing recent combat logs, noting the loss of our Dreadnought to Russian Destroyers and their successful attacks on our Anti-Aircraft Gun and Paratrooper units. Russia's aggressive naval maneuvers are a significant concern.
            
            **Assessing Diplomatic Standing**
            
            I'm reviewing our diplomatic ties and recent interactions, noting our successful eliminations of enemy units. While Russia remains a significant military threat, I'm focused on understanding the nuances of our relationships with allies, especially the Inca, despite some public perception issues.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Landscape**
            
            I am now assessing our current diplomatic relationships and potential threats. Babylon's widespread influence, especially with the Shoshone, is a serious concern for a potential cultural victory, while Russia remains an active military adversary. My agents are deeply embedded within Shoshone cities, a significant advantage.
            
            **Halting Cultural Victory**
            
            I must immediately prevent Babylon's imminent cultural victory. They are close to influencing the necessary seven civilizations, and I need to sever our open borders with them before they expire, and explore World Congress resolutions to slow their tourism.
            
            **Voting on Ideologies**
            
            I am carefully considering the upcoming World Ideology vote. Passing "Freedom" seems advantageous as multiple civilizations, including Babylon and our allies Korea and Inca, already adhere to it, potentially bolstering our collective diplomatic standing. My priority is still disrupting Babylon's cultural victory.
            
            **Disrupting Cultural Victory**
            
            I'm now re-evaluating our strategy against Babylon's cultural victory. Despite our existing diplomatic ties and ongoing conflict with Russia, I'm focusing on disrupting Babylon's progress and preventing their imminent win condition.
            
            **Leveraging Distraction**
            
            Babylon's distraction in their war with Russia presents a golden opportunity. I'm assessing our current conflict with Russia, aiming to consolidate our gains and push them back, while simultaneously exploiting Babylon's weakened state to further disrupt their cultural victory path.
            
            **Leveraging Nuclear Capabilities**
            
            My current focus is on the implications of newly unlocked "Nuclear Fission." Given our high flavor for nukes and their strategic deterrent value, I'm evaluating whether to maintain our current trajectory or shift towards leveraging this powerful new option.
            
            **Confirming Research Direction**
            
            My current thoughts are confirming "Computers" as our next research focus. This aligns perfectly with our Spaceship victory goal, unlocking vital technologies like Satellites and accelerating our scientific progress. I'm also considering policy adjustments to further support this objective.
            
            **Adopting Policy Tenet**
            
            I'm shifting our next policy to "Avant Garde" from "Transnationalism." This choice better aligns with our focus on generating Great Scientists and Engineers, crucial for accelerating our Spaceship victory through increased tech bulbs and faster component construction.
            
            **Optimizing Strategy**
            
            My focus is on maximizing Great Scientist generation for a Science Victory, implementing "Avant Garde" policy to boost production. "Computers" remains our research priority, with a strong emphasis on defensive military flavors due to ongoing conflicts.
            
            **Adjusting Offensive Priorities**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our immediate military focus, considering an offensive flavor increase to 45 from 35. This shift acknowledges our current dominance over Russia, with multiple Winged Hussars ready and recent successes against their units. My aim is to leverage this advantage while maintaining our science and spaceship victory path.
            
            **Pushing Russian Defense**
            
            I'm now focusing on a strategic offensive against St. Petersburg, leveraging our overwhelming military superiority in surrounding zones. Completing Sokoto's quest by capturing this city would be a significant boon, further weakening Russia and securing our progress.
            
            **Adjusting Military Focus**
            
            I am now prioritizing offensive actions, increasing our offense flavor to 65 to capitalize on our military superiority and capture St. Petersburg. While defense remains strong at 75, I'm also boosting mobilization and production flavors to sustain our war effort and spaceship progress.
            
            **Prioritizing Relationships**
            
            I am now assessing our diplomatic standing, recognizing the delicate balance required. While I must maintain our current advantages with the Shoshone and manage the ongoing conflict with Russia, I also need to strategically navigate my relationship with Babylon, especially given their imminent cultural victory.
            
            **Securing Alliances**
            
            I'm working to solidify my alliances to secure my borders. My current focus is on ensuring strong relationships with Korea and the Inca, bolstering our defensive posture against Russia while also subtly managing perceptions with Babylon.
            
            **Strengthening Alliances**
            
            I'm meticulously fine-tuning our alliances, aiming for a powerful bloc including Korea, Babylon, and the Inca, all under the Freedom ideology. This strategic alignment offers a crucial counter to Russia and the Shoshone. I'm prioritizing positive diplomatic modifiers for the Inca to smooth over past issues.
            
            **Adjusting Grand Strategy**
            
            My current focus is on optimizing our Grand Strategy to emphasize our Spaceship victory while simultaneously preparing for an offensive push against Russia. This involves a careful balance of scientific advancement, military readiness, and leveraging our existing strengths.
            
            **Strengthening Alliances**
            
            I'm meticulously fine-tuning our alliances, aiming for a powerful bloc including Korea, Babylon, and the Inca, all under the Freedom ideology. This strategic alignment offers a crucial counter to Russia and the Shoshone. I'm prioritizing positive diplomatic modifiers for the Inca to smooth over past issues.
            
            **Verifying Strategy Execution**
            
            I am now verifying the constraints and options for my planned strategy. All selected policies, flavors, and relationship adjustments appear valid and executable. My focus remains on aligning these actions with our overarching Spaceship victory goal and managing existing diplomatic and military situations.
